A Sapphire at Her Throat","volume":"婚后外传：蓝宝石舞会与迟到的短剧","anchor":"chapter-0217","paragraphs":["Mara wore the borrowed sapphire at her throat and discovered that Cassian could forget a crowded room completely. He stood in the doorway of the university gallery, looking at her as though the evening had been invented for his private benefit. \"Dear,\" she said, \"you are blocking an ambassador.\"","\"A regrettable constitutional crisis.\"","The ambassador laughed and slipped past with her aide. She was an Asian art collector as well as a diplomat, and she had lent the necklace for tonight's benefit. Mara would return it before leaving. A cop at the door had the loan details; a commissioner had merely come to dance. The difference mattered to the guest who thought an expensive title meant someone else would carry his umbrella.","The chancellor had invited half the town. An astronomer argued cheerfully with a biologist beside a plant clone grown for a teaching display. An anthropologist listened to their dispute, then pointed out that neither had eaten. An athlete holding a baby asked to join their table. The baby tried to creep beneath a chair, and its mother gently lifted it back. A former classmate brought them a burger. Its butcher supplier was working the food counter while a carter brought chairs through the old courtyard. One could be a consumer of beautiful art and still become desperately interested in supper.","Cassian had been asked to help a dramatist with a short, scripted court scene. The play contained an absolutist monarch, an unfortunate defendant and a devil with excellent shoes. Mara was willing to take a role too. She and Cassian would alternate introductions. A former acrobat played the messenger; a recovering addict, Eli, had designed the set. He had taken the work on the side while continuing his regular job. A college dropout enrolled in a new course had made the costumes. The king’s conservative comrade and a timid bureaucrat rehearsed their argument at the rear. The show could deviate from its lines if the audience laughed. Nobody had been asked to turn a biography into the evening's entertainment.","The gallery's colonial portrait made a stern background to the play. In the next room, a painting of Christ had been lent by a Christian college. A cardinal was among the visitors, attending for the scholarship fund rather than to inspect anyone's dancing. An anatomical drawing of the prefrontal cortex belonged to the science students’ display, far from the paintings. The cardinal rule at the food table was to keep glasses clear of the pictures. Rowan announced that this was the only commandment he had ever needed explained twice.","A broadcaster wanted a photograph of Mara beside the painting. His boss, a celebrity interviewer, wanted one without the nervous apprentice in it. Mara asked the adolescent apprentice to stand beside her instead. The boy had been born in the same neighbourhood as her mother. A timid attendee could learn more by doing a job than by being hidden whenever the result might be seen. The interviewer smiled stiffly for the camera.","The university's business school had sent a CEO, a chief operating officer and a broker to meet students. A British businessman made a joke about how all three might analyse the wine bill. \"Charming chap,\" said the businessman when the apprentice passed. The CEO handed him a glass of water. Her assistant was a former barber who had decided to undertake a career change. \"We all get ahead by practising something badly first,\" she said. \"Some of us merely pretend otherwise.\"","Mara saw the assistant's face brighten. Cassian watched her watching it, then took her hand. \"You have the look of a woman planning mischief.\"","\"I am being tame.\"","\"A disturbing costume.\"","At the workshop table, a craftsman showed a browser how a tiny conveyor moved painted scenery. Its little wheel could withstand repeated use; the paper passengers could not withstand careless fingers. A child pushed too hard, and one domino tipped into another. The student inventor laughed and reset them. The child became the discoverer of a tiny fox painted behind the train. Eli, a doer at heart, checked the pin before the next demonstration. An engineering classmate had led a peer-review session on the mechanism before the public demonstration. The students had checked one another’s work; today’s visitors were testing how easily a newcomer could use it.","The room had a crazy energy that some found unsettling. A critic tried to bully a performer into moving, then disparaged the display as childish. Eli asked whether that was supposed to refute its purpose. The critic looked surprised to be challenged by a man in paint-stained trousers. Cassian, whose aristocrat costume had been fitted so well that he barely needed to act, offered the critic his place in the children's queue. The man declined. Rowan whispered that a coward could always be identified by fear of a cardboard train.","A drunk guest argued about his coat. His friend took him aside so the quarrel would not degenerate into a scene. Cassian felt privileged to have Mara beside him throughout it. The play's director was desperate for a missing prop. The costume designer had enlarged a size zero jacket rather than force an actor into it. A militantly practical tailor, she worked on the side and hoped to enroll for formal training. An adolescent delegate from the costume group found it beneath a chair. A militant little doubter called the whole scene historically inaccurate. The dramatist agreed: it was a comedy. The pretend king's descendant did not actually need to inherit tonight's pudding. Its history was unimportant to the current joke. Cassian promised to try Rowan’s laugh sometime. The director would switch to a less solemn introduction before someone requested a genealogy lecture.","The play would support a wildlife refuge too. It was in part the reason the athletic team had come. Then Mara felt a faint touch beneath her hair. A man passing behind her had apologised for brushing her shoulder. She turned toward Cassian and saw his eyes change. The sapphire's setting remained at her throat. The centre stone was gone. The desolate little gap suddenly mattered more than her borrowed finery.","\"Once and for all,\" she said softly, \"I must stop calling these quiet evenings.\"","He did not smile. \"Tell me where he went.\""],"questions":[],"wordCount":1048,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0217\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：蓝宝石舞会与迟到的短剧</div><h1>1. A baby began to cry, and the athlete holding it moved into a quieter room. Her boss remained calmer than most of the guests. The Asian diplomat had lent the necklace knowing a crowd carried risk; that did not make her indifferent to its loss. The sapphire had belonged to her mother. Her account stopped there. Mara would not require a tragic history before taking care of something dear to its owner.","A broadcaster began filming the corridor. The cop asked him to step back so staff could move. The celebrity interviewer tried to present the loss as a dramatic discovery by his own assistant. The adolescent boy refused the wording. He had seen nothing until Mara told him. Being born in a wealthy household did not make the interviewer entitled to a more flattering version of events. The boy's classmate watched with fierce approval.","Cassian found the pale scarf beside the tiny conveyor display. The craftsman had been teaching a browser to reset the domino pieces, and a visitor had left the cloth on the rail. A burger wrapper lay beneath it. The butcher recognised the wrapping but could not identify its eater. The consumer who ate it was one of hundreds; the wrapping did not identify him. The carter, however, remembered a pale-scarfed man asking where the courtyard gate led. He pointed them toward it.","Mara followed with Cassian and the cop, leaving the crowd behind. On the stairs she passed an astronomer holding the plant clone for the biologist above the crush. The anthropologist had enlisted two students to clear a path. None had become an instant detective. They were simply keeping a fragile display and a few children out of the corridor. The chancellor took over the desk so the staff member could answer the police questions.","Outside, a drunk guest blocked the gate while arguing with the British businessman about a taxi. The businessman was trying to help and growing steadily less polite. A former barber brought water. The CEO and chief operating officer had called the man's companion. A broker attempted to broker an agreement that he should sit down. The drunk finally obeyed the barber, who had addressed him as a person rather than a problem to solve at volume.","His crazy attempt to deviate from the main route brought him toward the closed courtyard gate. A dark figure moved behind the catering cart. Mara saw a flash of blue at his wrist. The man had wrapped the sapphire in tissue and slipped it into a cuff. He saw her notice. His attempt to creep through the gate became a run. She caught the cart's canvas cover, pulled it across his path and stepped aside. Cassian reached him as he stumbled, keeping him clear of the stone edge. The cop took control. Mara did not have to turn a successful interruption into a fight.","The thief looked like a disappointed chap who had imagined a much easier evening. He was a doubter in the value of planning, Cassian observed later. Mara named Cassian discoverer of mislaid linen. The thief’s plan had a fatal flaw: he had counted on Mara noticing nothing. The thief did not appreciate the joke. The stone was recovered and would be checked before any claim that it was undamaged. A coward had chosen the quickest path and nearly hit a wall. His role as a defendant belonged to a future process, not to the play rehearsing inside.","Mara's fingers had started to tremble. She had not noticed until Cassian took them. He had no patience for the critic who called the chaos degenerate behaviour. The critic tried to bully the costume maker into silence. His work was practical, his voice militantly cheerful. He would not be shamed out of a useful task.","The dramatist said nobody would stay. A queue at the door refuted him. The director could delegate the curtain cue. The show planned by the dramatist could withstand a delay. He would switch to the shorter scripted scene and let each attendee choose whether to stay. navigation\"><button data-go=\"217\">← Previous</button><button data-go=\"219\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"x042c01","title":"1. A Sign Saying Heretic","volume":"婚后外传：旅馆里藏起来的礼物","anchor":"chapter-0220","paragraphs":["On the eve of the winter lodge's opening, Mara received a note asking her to wear something she could dance in. Her favourite foe in matters of quiet weekends was Rowan. She suspected him. Her lover claimed innocence with a look that would have convinced an uninformed stranger and nobody else. His lame excuse began to work against his claim. She chose a blue dress, then discovered him waiting with the pearl earrings she had thought lost.","\"You found them.\"","\"I am occasionally useful.\"","She kissed him before he could make usefulness sound like a small achievement. He grinned in response to her kiss.","Madame Roux owned the lodge. An entrepreneur, millionaire and terrifyingly competent matchmaker, she had offered rooms to the group restoring the old building. Its doors could dwarf a visitor, but its fireside made a city dweller feel instantly welcome. The inn was a practical earner in the village, and its reopening would bring work. A one-shot grant had helped repair the roof; regular guests would have to pay for everything that followed.","A caretaker had to hack a broken branch from the courtyard gate. The environmentally conscious host had reused old lanterns along the path. The gatekeeper admitted Mara with an enormous grin. A freshman waited inside, holding her coat as if it were a fragile flag. His foster mother was an educator who had helped him apply for college. A local entertainer taught him to bow, then ruined the lesson by making a face. The young lad willingly tried again. He moved his backpack so it would not get in the way. The kid laughed so hard he dropped the coat. Cassian caught it before the hem touched the snow.","The cook was beginning to fry onions for supper and promised that even a cake contest loser would leave well fed. At reception, a filer was working as a gatherer of family letters beside a genealogist's family chart. The chart recorded kin who had stayed at the lodge over generations. A foreigner could appear on it as naturally as a local child; the genealogist had been careful not to infer ethnic identity from a surname alone. A Hispanic cook pointed out the marriage line linking his grandmother to a village family. He and his husband, the gay entertainer, had brought supper rather than a speech.","Mara liked the company and distrusted the stage at the far end. A governor, a mayor and a retired magistrate were standing beside it. Leda, a lawmaker who called herself a very tired legislator tonight, had declined a ministerial dinner to come. The philanthropic landowner who lent the grounds looked as though she intended to unveil something. Mara was not going to become anybody's symbolic heroine merely because a borrowed title made a programme prettier.","Cassian recognised the narrowing of her eyes. \"I can explain.\"","\"You may need an excellent explanation.\"","Before he supplied one, a manicurist beckoned her toward a table. The woman had repaired hands for clients all week and wanted to offer a small pleasure to someone else. Mara accepted a clean cloth and warm water rather than a full treatment. Her working nails were short. Mara was willing to take the cloth and refresh herself before meeting everyone. A tape-measure hung from the wrist of the maid arranging cushions nearby. Her on-the-job training included measuring the costume seam, and would not pretend it happened by magic.","The lodge's oldest resident was a retired miner, a self-confessed Luddite who refused the new booking screen. He would still help an inventor test a chair lift. Ivo, the innovator behind its practical improvements, accepted the apparent contradiction with a grin. A humanist professor and a Marxist historian disagreed about the seating plan, then moved the same table. His analytic approach to cake distribution had already failed. The logician beside them asked whether two people agreeing on a table required agreement on history. Both said no.","An impressionist painter had made a little view of the garden. The giver of another picture was a former fowler, a hunter who now photographed the wild birds he once trapped. He had brought a peer-reviewed conservation article to explain the changing landscape to an interested layman. The article was not an account of his own virtue. It described the work of several people whose names he had marked carefully.","A visitor who was illiterate had asked a literate friend to read her letter aloud. The friend did so quietly. Near him, a meteorologist and a forecaster from the local station studied the clouds. The same woman could be both, she explained to a kid who thought the words named rival professions. Her weather talk was time-limited because dinner was nearly ready. The jury for the lodge's cake competition contained a juror with a serious face and icing on his sleeve. He promised to scrutinise every entry. The prize-giver had already eaten one corner of a cake and was trying to hide the evidence.","Mara saw a handwritten sign reading HEIR, HEROINE, HERETIC. Her suspicion hardened. Rowan appeared beneath it, dressed as a herald, and waved her forward. She did not move. A line about a killer had been crossed out below the sign. Mara began to despise its mysterious air. A fool could walk smiling onto a stage without first discovering what role she was being given. She had done enough of that before her marriage.","The retired fowler asked whether she disliked the picture. She assured him she did not. The hunter nodded toward the lodge's quiet side room, where her mother Elena sat with Noor. Elena had come for a short visit and would rest before the music. Mara's anger faltered. Her kin were here, and she had not expected to feel so moved by that.","The small painting was the embodiment of the garden as it had been before the repairs. Mara could regard the gift as a memory without knowing why everyone had gathered. The host had been liberal with cushions. The warmth was soothing. A neighbour promised to initiate the local reading group and commend its young helpers. He was its enthusiastic initiator, if not yet its organiser. It did not answer her question. She took Cassian's hand and led him firmly toward the empty corridor."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1048,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0220\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：旅馆里藏起来的礼物</div><h1>1.
